---

Reviewing changes made for GitHub issue #44 across:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-06-validate.md`

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. `step-08-report.md` still references the removed `coverage` field

**Confidence: 95**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-08-report.md`, line 69

The report template for the final compilation summary still uses `{coverage}` as a bare interpolation variable:

```
| **Exports Documented** | {documented_count} of {total_count} ({coverage}%) |
```

The old single `coverage` field has been removed from `metadata.json`. After this fix, there is no `stats.coverage` for the agent to populate this cell from. The agent will either leave the placeholder unfilled or compute an ambiguous coverage figure, since `metadata.json` now has two coverage fields (`public_api_coverage` and `total_coverage`) that mean different things.

This is a direct breakage: the report step reads from the compiled metadata, but the field it expects no longer exists. Fix by clarifying which coverage figure the report should display, or by expanding the row — for example:

```
| **Exports Documented** | {documented_count} public API ({public_api_coverage_pct}%) / {total_count} total ({total_coverage_pct}%) |
```

---

## Important Issues

### 2. `exports_documented` dropped from `step-06-validate.md` validation checklist

**Confidence: 88**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-06-validate.md`, line 181

The old validation line was:
```
- `stats.exports_documented` / `stats.exports_total` / `stats.coverage` are accurate
```

The new line is:
```
- `stats.exports_public_api` / `stats.exports_internal` / `stats.exports_total` are accurate
- `stats.public_api_coverage` and `stats.total_coverage` are correctly computed
```

`exports_documented` is still a field in the `metadata.json` template (line 131 of `skill-sections.md`) and it is still the numerator in both coverage formulas in `step-05-compile.md` (lines 97–98). But the validation step no longer explicitly directs the agent to verify that `exports_documented` is accurate. Since the two coverage ratios are both computed from this field, an incorrect `exports_documented` count would silently produce wrong coverage values that pass validation. Add `stats.exports_documented` back to the cross-check list.

### 3. `public_api_coverage` formula has no guard for the zero-denominator case

**Confidence: 85**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md`, line 97

The instruction reads:
```
`public_api_coverage`: `exports_documented / exports_public_api` (1.0 when all public API exports are documented)
```

There is no instruction for what to do when `exports_public_api` is 0, which happens for:
- Go packages (no single idiomatic entry point file; all exported symbols are public by capitalization convention)
- Rust binary crates (no `lib.rs`)
- Docs-only skills where no source was scanned

Division by zero is undefined, and without explicit guidance the agent will either produce `null`, `Infinity`, or default to 0.0, all of which are misleading. The `step-05-compile.md` instruction should specify the fallback, for example: "If `exports_public_api` is 0, set `public_api_coverage` to `null` and record a warning." The same applies to `total_coverage` when `exports_total` is 0.

### 4. `quick-skill` workflow retains the old schema and is not in sync

**Confidence: 90**

Files:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`, lines 77–80
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md`, lines 133–136
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md`, lines 125–126

The `quick-skill` workflow produces its own `metadata.json` using only the two old fields:

```json
"stats": {
  "exports_documented": "{number}",
  "exports_total": "{number}"
}
```

And `quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md` checks for exactly `stats.exports_documented` and `stats.exports_total` — nothing else. Skills produced by `quick-skill` will have a structurally different `stats` object than skills produced by `create-skill`. Any downstream tooling (test-skill, audit-skill, export-skill) that reads `metadata.json` stats uniformly will encounter missing fields on quick-skill outputs. Even if `quick-skill` is intentionally a lighter schema, it should at minimum not be actively inconsistent — it should either adopt the new fields with simplified population rules, or the schema difference should be explicitly documented so downstream steps know to handle both shapes.

---

## No Issues Found

- The `audit-skill` workflow (all 6 steps) contains no references to `stats.coverage`, `exports_total`, or `exports_documented`. It operates on the provenance map and extraction snapshots, not on `metadata.json` stats fields. No breakage there.
- The `test-skill/step-03-coverage-check.md` uses its own internal `exports_documented` as a local variable in its per-file JSON findings structure (line 99), which is entirely separate from the `metadata.json` stats field. No breakage there.
- The `test-skill` workflow's scoring formula at line 141 (`(documented / total_exports) * 100`) is also its own runtime variable, not a read from `metadata.json`. No breakage.

---

## Summary

| Severity | File | Issue |
|---|---|---|
| Critical | `create-skill/steps-c/step-08-report.md:69` | `{coverage}` placeholder references a field that no longer exists |
| Important | `create-skill/steps-c/step-06-validate.md:181` | `exports_documented` silently dropped from the cross-check instruction |
| Important | `create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md:97` | No division-by-zero guard when `exports_public_api` is 0 (Go, binary crates, docs-only) |
| Important | `quick-skill/` (3 files) | Old two-field stats schema not updated; structurally inconsistent with the new schema |

title:	Sparse checkout does not apply exclude patterns — use --no-cone mode
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	45
--
## Problem

`source-resolution-protocols.md` converts `include_patterns` from the brief to sparse-checkout directory roots, but has no mechanism for `exclude_patterns`. The clone fetches everything under included directories (e.g., all of `cognee/` including `cognee/tests/`, `cognee/alembic/`), then relies on post-checkout file filtering.

For repos where excluded directories contain many large files, this downloads unnecessary blobs and wastes bandwidth/time.

## Where it happens

- `data/source-resolution-protocols.md` — Remote Source Resolution, sparse-checkout section

## Current behavior

```bash
# Only include patterns are converted to sparse-checkout args
git -C $TEMP sparse-checkout set cognee
# Exclude patterns applied later as file-level filters
```

This checks out `cognee/tests/` (348 files) and `cognee/alembic/` (12 files) that are immediately filtered out.

## Proposed fix

Use `--no-cone` mode which natively supports negation patterns:

```bash
git -C $TEMP sparse-checkout set --no-cone \
  'cognee/**' \
  '!cognee/tests/**' \
  '!cognee/alembic/**' \
  '!**/test_*' \
  '!**/*_test.py'
```

`--no-cone` mode interprets patterns as gitignore-style rules where `!` prefix means exclude. This applies both include and exclude at the git level, avoiding unnecessary blob downloads.

### Protocol update needed in `source-resolution-protocols.md`:

1. After converting `include_patterns` to directory roots, also convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!`
2. When any `exclude_patterns` exist, use `--no-cone` instead of default cone mode
3. When no `exclude_patterns` exist, keep current cone mode behavior (faster)

## Impact

- Reduces clone size for repos with large test/build directories in scope
- Eliminates the post-checkout filtering step for exclude patterns
- No behavior change for briefs without exclude_patterns
```

> TOOL

     1→# Source Resolution Protocols
     2→
     3→## Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only)
     4→
     5→If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
     6→
     7→If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
     8→
     9→1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
    10→
    11→2. **Ephemeral shallow clone:** Clone the repository to a system temp path for AST access:
    12→
    13→   ```
    14→   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
    15→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
    16→   ```
    17→
    18→   **If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**
    19→
    20→   ```
    21→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
    22→   ```
    23→
    24→   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
    25→
    26→   ```
    27→   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
    28→   ```
    29→
    30→   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:
    31→
    32→   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).
    33→
    34→   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
    35→   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
    36→   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is
    37→
    38→   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**
    39→
    40→   ```
    41→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}
    42→   ```
    43→
    44→   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**
    45→
    46→   ```
    47→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
    48→   ```
    49→
    50→   Example transformation:
    51→   ```
    52→   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
    53→   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)
    54→   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)
    55→   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)
    56→   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
    57→   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
    58→   ```
    59→
    60→   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files.
    61→
    62→3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge/Deep Tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
    63→
    64→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
    65→
    66→   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
    67→
    68→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_repo}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
    69→
    70→   Proceed with Quick tier extraction strategy below. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
    71→
    72→**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the Gate 2 summary (Section 6), if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
    73→
    74→---
    75→
    76→## Version Reconciliation (all tiers, source mode only)
    77→
    78→**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:** skip this section — no source files exist to reconcile.
    79→
    80→After the source path is accessible (local path from step-01, or ephemeral clone from above), check whether the source contains a version identifier and reconcile it with `brief.version`. Look for the first matching version file in the resolved source path:
    81→
    82→- Python: `pyproject.toml` (`[project] version`), `setup.py` (`version=`), `__version__` in `__init__.py`
    83→- JavaScript/TypeScript: `package.json` (`"version"`)
    84→- Rust: `Cargo.toml` (`[package] version`)
    85→- Go: `go.mod` (module version if tagged)
    86→
    87→**If a source version is found AND it differs from `brief.version`:**
    88→
    89→⚠️ Warn the user: "Brief version ({brief.version}) differs from source version ({source_version}). Using source version ({source_version})."
    90→
    91→Update the working version in context to the source version. Record the mismatch in context for the evidence report (step-08).
    92→
    93→**If no version file is found or version cannot be extracted:** keep `brief.version` as-is. No warning needed.
    94→
    95→**If source is remote and accessed via Quick tier (gh_bridge, no local files):** attempt to read the version file via `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, "{version_file}")` for the primary version file of the detected language. If the read fails, keep `brief.version`.
    96→
```

  "new_string": "   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:\n\n   ```\n   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}\n   ```\n\n   **Mode selection:** Choose sparse-checkout mode based on whether `exclude_patterns` exist:\n\n   - **No `exclude_patterns`:** Use default **cone mode** (faster). Convert `include_patterns` to directory roots.\n   - **`exclude_patterns` present:** Use **`--no-cone` mode** which supports gitignore-style negation patterns (`!` prefix). This applies both include and exclude at the git level, avoiding unnecessary blob downloads.\n\n   #### Cone mode (no exclude patterns)\n\n   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:\n\n   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).\n\n   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)\n   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list\n   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is\n\n   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**\n\n   ```\n   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}\n   ```\n\n   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**\n\n   ```\n   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}\n   ```\n\n   Example transformation:\n   ```\n   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:\n   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)\n   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)\n   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)\n   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)\n   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)\n   ```\n\n   #### No-cone mode (exclude patterns present)\n\n   When `exclude_patterns` exist, use `--no-cone` mode to pass both include and exclude patterns directly as gitignore-style rules:\n\n   1. Convert `include_patterns` to gitignore-style patterns (keep as-is if they already use glob syntax like `cognee/**`)\n   2. Convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!` (e.g., `cognee/tests/**` → `!cognee/tests/**`)\n   3. Pass all patterns to sparse-checkout:\n\n   ```\n   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --no-cone {include_gitignore_patterns} {negated_exclude_patterns}\n   ```\n\n   Example transformation:\n   ```\n   Brief include_patterns:        Brief exclude_patterns:\n   cognee/**                      cognee/tests/**\n                                  cognee/alembic/**\n                                  **/test_*\n\n   sparse-checkout args (--no-cone):\n   'cognee/**' '!cognee/tests/**' '!cognee/alembic/**' '!**/test_*'\n   ```\n\n   **Note:** `--no-cone` mode is slower than cone mode for very large repositories but eliminates downloading excluded blobs entirely.\n\n   #### Post-checkout filtering\n\n   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files. When `--no-cone` mode was used, most exclude filtering is already done at the git level, but apply `exclude_patterns` as a final pass to catch any edge cases where gitignore pattern matching diverges from the brief's glob semantics."

No root-level CLAUDE.md exists; the project guidelines are captured in the workflow files themselves. I have everything needed for the review. Here is the full analysis.

---

## Review: `source-resolution-protocols.md` -- fix for issue #45 (sparse-checkout --no-cone mode)

Reviewing `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md` and its two dependent files.

---

## Critical Issues

### 1. --no-cone negation syntax is inverted -- excluded patterns will be INCLUDED, not excluded

**Confidence: 97**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md`, lines 72 and 87.

This is the most significant bug in the change. The document states:

> Convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!` (e.g., `cognee/tests/**` → `!cognee/tests/**`)

But in git's `--no-cone` mode, the sparse-checkout file uses gitignore-style pattern semantics where the polarity is **inclusion-by-default**. A pattern without `!` means "include this path." A pattern with `!` means "do NOT include this path" -- i.e., exclude it. So prepending `!` to an exclude pattern is the correct operation.

The critical error is missing the **mandatory leading include-all pattern**. In non-cone mode, the sparse-checkout file starts with nothing checked out. Without an initial wildcard, no files are included at all, and then the negation patterns have nothing to negate against. The canonical git pattern for "include everything except X" is:

```
/*
!X
```

For the specific case in the document (include `cognee/**`, exclude `cognee/tests/**`), the correct pattern set is:

```
cognee/**
!cognee/tests/**
!cognee/alembic/**
!**/test_*
```

This actually works correctly **as long as** there is at least one positive include pattern listed before the negations. The document's example does have `cognee/**` listed first, so for that specific example the output is correct.

However, the **prose instruction at line 71-72** is incomplete in a dangerous way:

> 1. Convert `include_patterns` to gitignore-style patterns (keep as-is if they already use glob syntax like `cognee/**`)
> 2. Convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!`
> 3. Pass all patterns to sparse-checkout

The instruction does not specify that positive patterns must come before negative ones in the argument list. Git processes patterns in order (last-writer-wins within a prefix group). If an AI agent constructs the argument list as negations-first then includes, the excludes will be overridden. The instruction must explicitly state: include_patterns first, then negated exclude_patterns.

Additionally, step 1 says "keep as-is if they already use glob syntax." But in `--no-cone` mode, patterns are anchored differently from cone mode. A pattern like `cognee/**` in no-cone mode matches `cognee/` and everything under it, which is correct. But a bare directory name like `cognee` (without `/**`) in no-cone mode does NOT recursively match subdirectories the way cone mode does. An agent following the instruction to "keep as-is" for a pattern like `src/core` (no trailing glob) would produce a broken pattern.

**Fix:** Strengthen the instructions to make ordering explicit and add the anchoring rule:

```
1. Convert include_patterns to gitignore-style patterns. Patterns without glob characters
   or a trailing `/` must have `/**` appended to match recursively
   (e.g., `cognee` → `cognee/**`; `cognee/**` → kept as-is).
2. Convert exclude_patterns to negation patterns by prepending `!`. Apply the same
   anchoring rule (e.g., `cognee/tests` → `!cognee/tests/**`).
3. ALWAYS list all include patterns BEFORE negated exclude patterns — git processes
   patterns in order and a negation can only suppress a prior inclusion.
4. Pass to sparse-checkout: include patterns first, then negated exclude patterns.
```

---

### 2. `extraction-patterns.md` Tier Degradation section references sparse-checkout without mentioning --no-cone

**Confidence: 85**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`, line 247.

The Tier Degradation Rules section contains this instruction:

> if `include_patterns` are specified, convert glob patterns to directory roots before passing to `sparse-checkout set` — `git sparse-checkout` expects directories, not globs. Use `--skip-checks` when any individual file paths (no glob characters) are present. Apply original glob patterns as file-level filters after checkout. See step-03-extract.md for the full conversion rules.

This is now stale and directly contradicts the new behavior in `source-resolution-protocols.md`. An AI agent reading only `extraction-patterns.md` (which it may do during tier degradation decision-making, since that is the first file loaded in step-03) will:

1. Always attempt cone-mode conversion (stripping glob patterns to directory roots).
2. Never consider `--no-cone` even when `exclude_patterns` are present.
3. Be directed to "see step-03-extract.md for the full conversion rules," but step-03-extract.md actually delegates to `source-resolution-protocols.md`.

The cross-reference chain is: `extraction-patterns.md` → "see step-03-extract.md" → step-03-extract.md loads `sourceResolutionData` which is `source-resolution-protocols.md`. This is two levels of indirection during a degradation path, which is fragile.

**Fix:** Update line 247 of `extraction-patterns.md` to match the new dual-mode logic:

```
if `include_patterns` are specified, apply mode selection: if `exclude_patterns` are
absent, use cone mode (convert include_patterns to directory roots; use `--skip-checks`
for individual file paths); if `exclude_patterns` are present, use `--no-cone` mode
(pass gitignore-style patterns with `!`-prefixed excludes). See source-resolution-protocols.md
for the full conversion rules.
```

---

## Important Issues

### 3. Edge case: mismatched pattern types between include_patterns and exclude_patterns are unaddressed

**Confidence: 82**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md`, lines 67-90.

The no-cone section handles the example where include_patterns are glob patterns (`cognee/**`) and exclude_patterns are also glob patterns (`cognee/tests/**`, `**/test_*`). But the document gives no guidance for the case where:

- `include_patterns` contains a bare directory name with no glob characters (e.g., `src/core`) AND `exclude_patterns` is non-empty (triggering --no-cone mode).

In cone mode (no excludes), the document correctly instructs converting `src/core/**/*.py` to the directory root `src/core`. In `--no-cone` mode, an AI agent following step 1 ("keep as-is if they already use glob syntax") would pass `src/core` literally. In `--no-cone` mode, `src/core` matches only the file literally named `src/core` at the repo root, not the directory contents. The pattern needs to be `src/core/**` or `/src/core/` to match directory contents.

This will silently produce an empty or wrong checkout that the agent will then process with the post-checkout glob filter, potentially surfacing as an unexplained empty extraction inventory rather than a clear error.

**Fix:** Add to the no-cone section, step 1:

> For each include pattern, if it contains no glob characters and does not end with `/`, append `/**` to make it match directory contents recursively (e.g., `src/core` → `src/core/**`). If it already contains glob characters, keep as-is.

---

### 4. step-03-extract.md does not need updating -- but the delegation chain has an existing gap

**Confidence: 80**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, line 82.

Step-03-extract.md at line 82 says:

> Load `{sourceResolutionData}` completely. Follow the **Remote Source Resolution** protocol for Forge/Deep tiers (ephemeral clone, sparse-checkout, cleanup)...

This correctly delegates entirely to `source-resolution-protocols.md`, so step-03-extract.md itself does not need a --no-cone mention. However, the prior concern (issue #2 above) applies: an agent that reaches the Tier Degradation path via `extraction-patterns.md` gets stale cone-mode-only instructions and is told to "see step-03-extract.md" rather than `source-resolution-protocols.md` directly. The fix belongs in `extraction-patterns.md`, not here.

---

## Summary

| # | File | Severity | Confidence |
|---|------|----------|------------|
| 1 | `source-resolution-protocols.md` L67-90 | Critical | 97 |
| 2 | `extraction-patterns.md` L247 | Important | 85 |
| 3 | `source-resolution-protocols.md` L67-90 | Important | 82 |
| 4 | `step-03-extract.md` L82 | Informational | 80 |

**Issue #1** (pattern ordering and anchoring omission in no-cone instructions) is the highest priority. The negation mechanics are technically correct in the given example but the prose instructions are incomplete enough that an AI agent following them in a non-example scenario will produce silently wrong checkouts. Issue #2 (stale Tier Degradation text) creates a real contradiction between the two data files that the same agent reads in sequence. Issue #3 (bare directory names in no-cone mode) is a concrete edge case that will cause empty inventories with no diagnostic output.

Sources:
- [Git - git-sparse-checkout Documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-sparse-checkout)
- [Git - sparse-checkout Documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/sparse-checkout)

    37→   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:
    38→
    39→   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).
    40→
    41→   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
    42→   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
    43→   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is
    44→
    45→   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**
    46→
    47→   ```
    48→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}
    49→   ```
    50→
    51→   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**
    52→
    53→   ```
    54→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
    55→   ```
    56→
    57→   Example transformation:
    58→   ```
    59→   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
    60→   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)
    61→   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)
    62→   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)
    63→   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
    64→   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
    65→   ```
    66→
    67→   #### No-cone mode (exclude patterns present)
    68→
    69→   When `exclude_patterns` exist, use `--no-cone` mode to pass both include and exclude patterns directly as gitignore-style rules:
    70→
    71→   1. Convert `include_patterns` to gitignore-style patterns. If a pattern contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) and does not end with `/`, append `/**` to match directory contents recursively (e.g., `cognee` → `cognee/**`; `cognee/**` → kept as-is).
    72→   2. Convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!`. Apply the same anchoring rule (e.g., `cognee/tests` → `!cognee/tests/**`; `cognee/tests/**` → `!cognee/tests/**`; `**/test_*` → `!**/test_*`).
    73→   3. **CRITICAL:** List all include patterns BEFORE negated exclude patterns — git processes patterns in order and a negation can only suppress a prior inclusion.
    74→   4. Pass to sparse-checkout — include patterns first, then negated exclude patterns:
    75→
    76→   ```
    77→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --no-cone {include_gitignore_patterns} {negated_exclude_patterns}
    78→   ```
    79→
    80→   Example transformation:
    81→   ```
    82→   Brief include_patterns:        Brief exclude_patterns:
    83→   cognee/**                      cognee/tests/**
    84→                                  cognee/alembic/**
    85→                                  **/test_*
    86→
    87→   sparse-checkout args (--no-cone):
    88→   'cognee/**' '!cognee/tests/**' '!cognee/alembic/**' '!**/test_*'
    89→   ```
    90→
    91→   **Note:** `--no-cone` mode is slower than cone mode for very large repositories but eliminates downloading excluded blobs entirely.
    92→
    93→   #### Post-checkout filtering
    94→
    95→   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files. When `--no-cone` mode was used, most exclude filtering is already done at the git level, but apply `exclude_patterns` as a final pass to catch any edge cases where gitignore pattern matching diverges from the brief's glob semantics.
    96→
    97→3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge/Deep Tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
    98→
    99→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
   100→
   101→   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
   102→
   103→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_repo}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and update `source_repo` in your brief to the local path."
```

title:	Module-level re-exports not traced by AST extraction
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	46
--
## Problem

When a top-level `__init__.py` exports a module (not a function directly), the extraction step cannot find the actual function definition. The workflow assumes each public export maps to a readable function definition file, but Python module re-exports break this assumption.

## Where it happens

- `steps-c/step-03-extract.md` — Section 4, extraction strategy
- `data/extraction-patterns.md` — Python patterns section

## Example

`cognee/__init__.py` exports:
```python
from .api.v1.delete import delete
from .api.v1.datasets.datasets import datasets
```

But `cognee/api/v1/delete/delete.py` is empty — `delete` is actually a module namespace whose functions are re-exported via `cognee/api/v1/delete/__init__.py`.

Similarly, `datasets` is a class-like namespace, not a direct function. The AST pattern `def $NAME($$$PARAMS)` cannot find these.

## Current behavior

- AST scan finds nothing for `delete` → export is undocumented or manually inferred
- The extraction inventory has a gap for module-level re-exports
- The compiled SKILL.md lists these exports with only `[AST:cognee/api/v1/delete/]` (directory citation, not line-level)

## Proposed fix

Add a "follow re-export" protocol to the extraction step:

1. After initial AST extraction, identify top-level exports from `__init__.py` (or equivalent entry point)
2. For each export that resolved to a **module import** (not a direct function):
   - Read the module's `__init__.py` to find the actual re-exported symbols
   - Trace each re-exported symbol to its definition file
   - Run AST extraction on those definition files
3. Cite as `[AST:{actual_definition_file}:L{line}]`

### Python-specific detection:

```python
# Module re-export pattern (import is a directory, not a .py file)
from .api.v1.delete import delete  # delete/ is a directory → follow __init__.py

# Direct function import (no follow needed)
from .api.v1.add.add import add    # add.py exists with def add()
```

The heuristic: if the import path resolves to a directory (contains `__init__.py`) rather than a `.py` file, follow the `__init__.py` re-exports.

## Impact

- Incomplete extraction for libraries using Python namespace patterns
- Affects `delete`, `datasets`, and similar module-level exports
- Most Python libraries with clean public APIs use this pattern extensively
```

  "new_string": "### Re-Export Tracing (Python)\n\nAfter initial AST extraction, some top-level exports may resolve to **module imports** rather than direct function definitions. This is common in Python libraries that use `__init__.py` re-exports for a clean public API.\n\n**Detection heuristic:** For each top-level export from `__init__.py` (or equivalent entry point), check if the import path resolves to a directory (contains `__init__.py`) rather than a `.py` file with a matching `def` or `class`. If the initial AST scan found no function/class definition for a known public export, it is likely a module re-export.\n\n**Tracing protocol:**\n\n1. Read the entry point file (e.g., `{package}/__init__.py`) and extract all `from .X import Y` statements\n2. For each import where Y was NOT found by the initial AST scan:\n   - Check if the import path resolves to a directory (e.g., `{package}/api/v1/delete/` exists with `__init__.py`)\n   - If directory: read its `__init__.py` to find the actual re-exported symbol\n   - Trace the symbol to its definition file and run AST extraction on that file\n3. Cite the actual definition location: `[AST:{definition_file}:L{line}]`\n\n**Examples:**\n\n```python\n# Module re-export — follow required\nfrom .api.v1.delete import delete    # delete/ is a directory → read delete/__init__.py\n\n# Direct function import — no follow needed\nfrom .api.v1.add.add import add      # add.py exists with def add()\n```\n\n**Scope limit:** Only trace re-exports for symbols listed in the top-level entry point's public API. Do not recursively trace beyond one level of `__init__.py` indirection. If a re-export cannot be resolved after one level, record it with a `[SRC:{entry_point}:L{line}]` citation (T1-low) from the import statement itself.\n\n**Other languages:** JS/TS barrel files (`index.ts` with `export { X } from './module'`) follow the same principle — trace the re-export to the definition file. Rust `pub use` and Go package-level re-exports are less common but follow the same heuristic when encountered.\n\n---\n\n## Tier Degradation Rules"
}
```

I now have everything I need. Let me review the heading structure of the extraction-patterns.md file more carefully to answer question 1.

Looking at the heading hierarchy in `extraction-patterns.md`:

- `#` — "Extraction Patterns by Tier" (line 1)
- `##` — Major sections: "Quick Tier", "Forge Tier", "Deep Tier", "AST Extraction Protocol", "Tier Degradation Rules" (lines 3, 27, 52, 67, 264)
- `###` — Subsections within those: "Strategy", "Confidence", "Supported Patterns", "Decision Tree", "Safety Valve", "MCP Tool Usage", "CLI Streaming Fallback", "YAML Rule Recipes by Language", "Re-Export Tracing (Python)", "Remote Source at Forge/Deep Tier", etc.

The new "Re-Export Tracing (Python)" section at line 233 is a `###` heading. Its siblings — "YAML Rule Recipes by Language", "Decision Tree", "Safety Valve", "MCP Tool Usage (Preferred)", "CLI Streaming Fallback" — are all `###` headings. The new section is correctly leveled.

Now I have a complete picture. Here is the full review.

---

Reviewing the two changed files against the project's existing conventions, the heading hierarchy in `extraction-patterns.md`, and the wider workflow ecosystem.

---

## Summary of Changes Reviewed

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` — new "Re-Export Tracing (Python)" subsection at lines 233-261
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` — new "Re-export tracing (Forge/Deep only)" paragraph at lines 118-121

---

## Critical Issues

None found.

---

## Important Issues

### 1. Heading level is correct — no issue here

Question 1 was whether the new `###` heading was wrong. It is not. Every subsection inside `## AST Extraction Protocol` uses `###`: "Decision Tree", "Safety Valve", "MCP Tool Usage (Preferred)", "CLI Streaming Fallback", "YAML Rule Recipes by Language" are all `###`. The new "Re-Export Tracing (Python)" at line 233 follows this exact pattern. No change needed.

---

### 2. Detection heuristic is underspecified for `from X import *` — Confidence: 85

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`, lines 237-238

The heuristic says: "check if the import path resolves to a directory rather than a `.py` file with a matching `def` or `class`. If the initial AST scan found no function/class definition for a known public export, it is likely a module re-export."

This heuristic will silently fail — producing no tracing, and therefore a gap in the extraction inventory — for two common real-world patterns:

**`from .api import *`** — This is a wildcard re-export. The symbol is in scope at the top-level `__init__.py` but there is no per-symbol `from .X import Y` line to match. The tracing protocol at step 1 (lines 241-246) reads "extract all `from .X import Y` statements", which does not match a star-import. The agent will skip it. A public API surface exposed via `__all__` combined with `import *` chains is particularly common in Python packages that use this pattern for namespace consolidation.

**Conditional imports** (`try: from .fast import X / except ImportError: from .slow import X`) — The tracing protocol reads the entry point's import statements, but if both branches define the same symbol, the agent will likely trace only the first one it finds, or fail to match either if the AST scan produced a result from one branch but the tracing protocol encounters the try/except structure.

The protocol gives no handling instruction for either case. Because the stated goal is zero hallucination and every public export must have a provenance citation, an unhandled star-import or conditional import becomes a silent gap.

Suggested fix for the "Detection heuristic" paragraph — add a fallback rule for unresolvable patterns:

> If the import statement is a star-import (`from .X import *`) or a conditional import (`try`/`except`), the symbol cannot be reliably traced via this protocol. Record it with `[SRC:{entry_point}:L{line}]` (T1-low) and a note: "star/conditional import — manual trace required."

This keeps the zero-hallucination principle intact and is consistent with the fallback citation format already used in the scope-limit sentence (line 258).

---

### 3. The "Re-export tracing" paragraph in step-03-extract.md references the protocol by section name "Re-Export Tracing" but the section heading in extraction-patterns.md uses the title "Re-Export Tracing (Python)" — Confidence: 80

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`, line 120

The step says: "Follow the **Re-Export Tracing** protocol in `{extractionPatternsData}`"

The actual heading in the data file is `### Re-Export Tracing (Python)` (line 233). This is a markdown instruction file read by an AI agent, so the agent will search for the named section. The mismatch between "Re-Export Tracing" and "Re-Export Tracing (Python)" creates ambiguity — especially because the "Other languages" paragraph at the bottom of the section (line 260) extends coverage to JS/TS and Rust. An agent reading step-03 might (correctly) conclude the protocol applies to all languages, then search for a section called "Re-Export Tracing" and fail to locate "Re-Export Tracing (Python)" or, conversely, apply it only to Python because of the parenthetical.

Either the step-03 reference should be updated to match the exact heading — "the **Re-Export Tracing (Python)** protocol" — or the heading in extraction-patterns.md should be broadened to `### Re-Export Tracing` since the section already covers JS/TS barrel files and Rust `pub use` in its final paragraph. The latter is arguably better because the protocol's multi-language note is currently buried at the end and the heading suggests Python-only.

---

### 4. The one-level tracing limit is sound for common cases, but the fallback citation is ambiguous — Confidence: 82

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`, line 258

The scope limit reads: "If a re-export cannot be resolved after one level, record it with a `[SRC:{entry_point}:L{line}]` citation (T1-low) from the import statement itself."

The variable `{entry_point}` is undefined at this point in the document. Earlier in the tracing protocol (line 241), the entry point is described as "e.g., `{package}/__init__.py`", which uses a different placeholder `{package}`. These are both prose-level template tokens, not frontmatter variables, so they won't be substituted at runtime — but the inconsistency (`{entry_point}` vs `{package}/__init__.py`) could confuse an agent deciding what file path to use in the citation.

The scope-limit sentence should use the same concrete example form used earlier in the section, e.g.:

> "record it with a `[SRC:{package}/__init__.py:L{line}]` citation (T1-low)"

This is consistent with the citation examples already in the file (`[AST:{definition_file}:L{line}]`, `[SRC:{entry_point}:L{line}]` at step-03 line 36) — but within this single section, using two different placeholder styles for the same concept is a readability hazard.

---

### 5. The Quick tier reads entry points but gets no re-export tracing — the gap is real but the scope decision is correct — Confidence: 40 (below threshold, noted for completeness)

Question 4 asked whether the Quick tier also needs this. After reading `step-03-quick-extract.md` in full: the Quick tier reads `__init__.py` at step 3 and extracts "the `__all__` list, top-level function/class definitions" via pattern matching. It has no AST, so it cannot detect whether a name in `__all__` is locally defined or re-exported from a subdirectory — it simply takes whatever is visible in the file text. The Quick tier's baseline confidence is already T1-low across the board, so a missed re-export does not represent a confidence regression from the tier's stated capabilities. The limitation is implicit in "surface-level reading only." This does not need a fix, but could be acknowledged in a note if desired.

---

### 6. `update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md` is not updated — Confidence: 88

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`

This file references the same `{extractionPatternsData}` (line 86) and explicitly says "Load and follow the **AST Extraction Protocol** from `{extractionPatternsData}`." It performs AST extraction on changed files at Forge/Deep tier using the same protocol.

If an update run touches a Python package that uses module-level re-exports, the re-extract step will encounter exactly the same extraction gap that issue #46 describes — a symbol that exists at the entry point but wasn't found by the initial AST scan on the changed file. The fix in step-03-extract.md was not mirrored here.

The update-skill re-extract step operates on changed files only, so the tracing protocol would need a small adaptation: instead of "check the top-level entry point for symbols not found by AST extraction," the wording would be "for each changed file, after AST extraction, check if any symbols from that file's corresponding entry point are unresolved re-exports." The data file already contains the full tracing protocol, so only a reference paragraph analogous to the one added to step-03-extract.md (lines 118-121) needs to be added to step-03-re-extract.md's "Extract Changed Files" section (lines 98-113).

---

title:	Step 03c root page detection threshold is subjective — needs concrete heuristic
state:	OPEN
author:	armelhbobdad (Armel BOBDA)
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	47
--
## Problem

Step 03c (Fetch Docs) instructs the agent to check if a fetched URL is a "documentation root with minimal useful API content" using the threshold "fewer than 500 characters of meaningful text excluding navigation/menu items." This is subjective — distinguishing "meaningful text" from "navigation" in scraped markdown requires judgment that varies between runs and agents.

## Where it happens

- `steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md` — Section 3, Subpage discovery (root URL detection)

## Current behavior

> "check if the returned content is a documentation root with minimal useful API content (e.g., mostly navigation links, fewer than 500 characters of meaningful text excluding navigation/menu items)"

During the cognee skill compilation, the root page (`docs.cognee.ai/`) contained a mix of navigation links and some content blocks (descriptions of sections). Whether this qualifies as "minimal useful API content" is ambiguous.

## Proposed fix

Define a concrete, deterministic heuristic. For example:

### Option A — Link density ratio:
```
meaningful_lines = lines that do NOT match: /^\s*\[.*\]\(.*\)\s*$/
total_lines = all non-empty lines
if meaningful_lines / total_lines < 0.3:
    → root page detected, trigger subpage discovery
```

### Option B — Word count after stripping links:
```
Strip all markdown links: [text](url) → remove entirely
Strip all image refs: ![alt](src) → remove entirely
Count remaining words
if word_count < 100:
    → root page detected
```

### Option C — API content indicators:
```
Look for code blocks (```), parameter tables (|---|), function signatures (def/function/fn)
if api_indicators == 0:
    → root page detected (no API content, likely navigation-only)
```

Option C is the most semantically meaningful — if the page has zero code/API content, it's definitely a navigation root regardless of text length.

## Impact

- Low severity — the current subjective threshold works in practice
- But it produces non-deterministic behavior across different agent implementations
- A concrete heuristic would make the workflow reproducible
```

    83→

    85→
    86→After fetching a URL, check if the returned content is a documentation root with minimal useful API content (e.g., mostly navigation links, fewer than 500 characters of meaningful text excluding navigation/menu items). This is common with modern documentation sites (Mintlify, Docusaurus, ReadTheDocs, GitBook) that render API content on subpages.
    87→
    88→**If a root URL with minimal content is detected:**
    89→
    90→1. **Attempt sitemap/map discovery:** Use whatever discovery tool is available:
    91→   - Firecrawl: `firecrawl_map({url})` to discover all subpages
    92→   - Manual: try fetching `{url}/sitemap.xml` and parsing URLs from it
    93→   - Crawl: if a crawl tool is available, use it with depth=1 on the root URL
    94→   - If no discovery tool is available, keep the root page content as-is and continue
    95→
    96→2. **Filter discovered URLs by relevance:** From the discovered subpage list, select the most relevant pages by searching for API-related terms in the URL path or title (e.g., `api`, `reference`, `quickstart`, `setup`, `config`, `getting-started`, `guide`, `sdk`, `methods`, `functions`). Exclude pages that are clearly non-API content (e.g., `blog`, `changelog`, `pricing`, `about`, `careers`).
    97→
    98→3. **Fetch top subpages:** Fetch up to **10** of the most relevant subpages. For each:
    99→   - Use the same web fetching tool as the root URL
   100→   - Store with the subpage URL as provenance: `[EXT:{subpage-url}]`
   101→   - If a subpage fetch fails, skip it and continue
   102→
   103→4. **Rate limiting:** If rate limiting (HTTP 429) is encountered during subpage fetching, stop discovery for this root URL. Keep results collected so far. Log: "Subpage discovery stopped due to rate limiting."
   104→
   105→**If ALL URLs fail (including any subpage fetches):** Log warning: "No documentation could be fetched. Proceeding without T3 content." Skip to section 7 (auto-proceed).
   106→
   107→### 4. Extract API Information from Fetched Content
```

> AGENT

The fix is to replace the subjective threshold with a concrete heuristic. Option C (API content indicators) is the most deterministic — combined with a link-density check for robustness.

This is common with modern documentation sites (Mintlify, Docusaurus, ReadTheDocs, GitBook) that render API content on subpages.",
  "new_string": "After fetching a URL, apply the following heuristic to detect documentation root pages that contain no useful API content. This is common with modern documentation sites (Mintlify, Docusaurus, ReadTheDocs, GitBook) that render API content on subpages.\n\n**Root page detection heuristic — a page is a root if BOTH conditions are true:**\n\n1. **Zero API content indicators:** The fetched markdown contains none of: fenced code blocks (`` ``` ``), parameter tables (`|---|`), or function signature patterns (`def `, `function `, `fn `, `func `, `export `).\n2. **High link density:** More than 70% of non-empty lines are markdown links (matching `[text](url)` with no other substantive content on the line).\n\nIf only one condition is true, treat the page as having partial content — keep it as-is and do NOT trigger subpage discovery."
}
```
